u=0
, and fix a tame
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
throughout the subsection.

By Lemma
8.12
,
M
decomposes as an
\mathcal{R}
-module as a direct sum
\hat{F}\oplus\hat{N}
, where
\hat{F}
and
\hat{N}
satisfy the requirements
as modules over
\mathcal{R}
; however it is not clear a priori whether one can achieve that
\hat{F}
and
\hat{N}
are tame sub-
\mathcal{A}
-modules of
M
.
For
\hat{N}
, which is narrow, being a sub-
\mathcal{A}
-module of
M
is equivalent to the requirement that for all
i\geq 1
, the map
\mathfrak{y}_{i}\cdot-\colon M\to M
restricts to the zero map on
\hat{N}
.

We fix a decomposition
M\cong\hat{F}\oplus\hat{N}
over
\mathcal{R}
as above throughout the subsection.

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
\underline{a}=\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{i_{0}}\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathfrak{y}_{h}^{i_{h}}
be a monomial in
\mathcal{A}
, with
h\geq 0
and
0\leq i_{j}<d_{j}
for
0\leq j\leq h
, and denote
k=\sum_{j=0}^{h}D_{j}i_{j}
, so that
\underline{a}
has weight
-2k
.
Let
\nu\in M
be an element of weight at most
-k
, and assume that
\mathfrak{y}_{j}^{d_{j}-i_{j}}\nu=0
for all
0\leq j\leq h
. Then
\nu
is in the image of the map
\underline{a}\cdot-\colon M\to M
.

Proof.

The statement is a dualisation of the third condition for
M
to be tame from Definition
8.5
, and we spell out the details.

By the definition of
\mathcal{A}
, we have an inclusion of weighted sub-
\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces of
M
of the form
\mathrm{Im}(\underline{a}\cdot-)\subseteq\ker(\mathfrak{y}_{j}^{d_{j}-i_{j}}\cdot-)
, for every
0\leq j\leq h
. The statement of the lemma is equivalent to this inclusion being an equality in weights at most
-k
. By dualising and a rank-nullity argument, it suffices to prove the inclusion
\ker(a\cdot-)\subseteq\sum_{j=0}^{h}\mathrm{Im}(\mathfrak{y}_{j}^{d_{j}-i_{j}}\cdot-)
sub-
\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces of
M^{\vee}
in weights at least
k
.

Since
M^{\vee}
is isomorphic to
M
, it is also tame, and in particular
M^{\vee}_{\geq k}
agrees with a free
\mathcal{A}
-module in weights at least
-k
. Thus, if
z\in\ker(\underline{a}\cdot-)\subseteq M^{\vee}
has weight at least
k
, then in particular
z\in M^{\vee}_{\geq k}
; lifting
z
to a (unique) element
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
along a map
\psi_{k}\colon\mathcal{F}_{k}\to M^{\vee}
as in Definition
8.5
, we have that
\underline{a}\tilde{z}=0\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
, and since
\mathcal{F}_{k}
is free, this implies that
\tilde{z}
is in
\sum_{j=0}^{h}\mathrm{Im}(\mathfrak{y}_{j}^{d_{j}-i_{j}}\cdot-)\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{k}
; projecting along
\psi_{k}
to
M^{\vee}
we obtain
z\in\sum_{j=0}^{h}\mathrm{Im}(\mathfrak{y}_{j}^{d_{j}-i_{j}}\cdot-)\subseteq M^{\vee}
.
∎

Notation 8.16
.

We fix a decomposition of
\hat{N}
as a direct sum of cyclic modules
\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathopen{}\left(\mathbb{F}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{c_{j}})\right)\mathclose{}[m_{j}]
, where
r=|\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{y}_{0}}(\hat{N})|
. We denote by
\nu_{j}\in\hat{N}
the generator of the
j
th
cyclic summand; by Lemma
8.12
,
\nu_{j}
has weight
m_{j}>-d_{0}
.

Lemma 8.17
.

Let
\nu\in\hat{N}
be an element of weight
m\in]{-d_{0}},{D_{1}-d_{0}}[
generating a
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
-bar of
\hat{N}
of size
1\leq c<d_{1}
(i.e.
\nu=\nu_{j}
for some
j
). Then there exists an element
\bar{w}\in M
of weight
m
, such that
\bar{w}
is in the image of
\mathfrak{y}_{0}\cdot-\colon M\to M
, and moreover
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{c}\bar{w}=0
and
\mathfrak{y}_{i}\bar{w}=\mathfrak{y}_{i}\nu
for all
i\geq 1
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.12
we have
m\in]{(c-2)d_{0}},{cd_{0}}[
.
First, we prove by induction on
h\geq 0
that there exists an element
w_{h}\in\mathrm{Im}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}\cdot-)
of weight
m
and such that
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{c}w_{h}=0
and
\mathfrak{y}_{i}w_{h}=\mathfrak{y}_{i}\nu
for all
1\leq i\leq h
. For
h=0
we just take
w_{0}=0
. Let now
h>0
and suppose that we have proved the existence of
w_{h-1}
.

Let
z=\mathfrak{y}_{h}(\nu-w_{h-1})
; we observe that
z
has weight
m-2D_{h}
and is in the kernel of any of the maps
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{c}\cdot-
,
\mathfrak{y}_{1}\cdot-
,
\dots
,
\mathfrak{y}_{h-1}\cdot-
and
\mathfrak{y}_{h}^{d_{h+1}-1}\cdot-
(the last one because
z
is a multiple of
\mathfrak{y}_{h}
in
M
); we want to argue that
z
has the form
\underline{a}z^{\prime}
for some
z^{\prime}\in M
, where
\underline{a}
is the monomial
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-c}\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{d_{2}-1}\dots\mathfrak{y}_{h-1}^{d_{h}-1}\mathfrak{y}_{h}
, of weight

-2(D_{1}-cd_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}(D_{i+1}-D_{i})+D_{h})=-2(2D_{h}-cd_{0}).

For this we apply Lemma
8.15
, checking the inequality
m-2D_{h}\leq cd_{0}-2D_{h}
, which holds (even strictly) since
m<cd_{0}
.
We now set

w_{h}=w_{h-1}+\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-c}\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{d_{2}-1}\dots\mathfrak{y}_{h-1}^{d_{h}-1}z^{\prime}
, and readily check that it satisfies the required properties.

Since
M
is tame, it is supported in
]{-D_{h}},D_{h}[
for some
h
: we then set
\bar{w}=w_{h-1}
, which just as
\nu
is killed by all variables
\mathfrak{y}_{i}
with
i\geq h
.
∎

Notation 8.18
.

Let
A
be an algebra and let
a\in A
. For an
A
-module
M
we denote by
M\mathopen{}\left\{a\right\}\mathclose{}\subset M
the submodule of
a
-torsion elements. Both assignments
M\mapsto M\mathopen{}\left\{a\right\}\mathclose{}
and
M\mapsto M/M\mathopen{}\left\{a\right\}\mathclose{}
extend naturally to functors
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.

Proof of Proposition
8.14
.

Recall Notation
8.16
. For each
1\leq j\leq r
fix an element
\bar{w}_{j}\in M
as in Lemma
8.17
, corresponding to the element
\nu_{j}\in\hat{N}
.
Let
\bar{N}
be the sub-
\mathcal{R}
-module of
M
generated by the elements
\nu_{j}-\bar{w}_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq r
. Then, for every
i\geq 1
, the map
\mathfrak{y}_{i}\cdot-\colon M\to M
vanishes identically on
\bar{N}
, as it is a map of
\mathcal{R}
-modules and it vanishes on the
\mathcal{R}
-generators of
\bar{N}
. It follows that
\bar{N}
is a sub-
\mathcal{A}
-module of
M
. Moreover, since by the assertion of Lemma
8.17
each generator of
\bar{N}
differs from the corresponding generator of
\hat{N}
by a
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
-multiple, we have that
M
splits also as the
\mathcal{R}
-module direct sum
\hat{F}\oplus\bar{N}
.

The above reasoning also implies that
\bar{N}
and
\hat{N}
are isomorphic
\mathcal{R}
-modules; as
\hat{N}
is
\mathcal{R}
-self-dual, then so is
\bar{N}
. Indeed, the
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
-barcodes of
\bar{N}
and
\bar{N}^{\vee}
are obtained from the
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
-barcodes of
M
and
M^{\vee}
, respectively, by forgetting all bars of size
d_{1}
. Since for all
i\geq 1
the element
\mathfrak{y}_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
acts trivially on both
\bar{N}
and
\bar{N}^{\vee}
(both modules are “narrow”) we also have that
\bar{N}\cong\bar{N}^{\vee}
as
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

The inclusion of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\bar{N}\hookrightarrow M
dualises to a surjection of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
M^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow\bar{N}^{\vee}
; we precompose this with the
\mathcal{A}
-linear isomorphism
M\cong M^{\vee}
and define
F\subset M
as the kernel of the composition: by construction
F
is a sub-
\mathcal{A}
-module of
M
. We notice that
F
is isomorphic to
(\hat{F})^{\vee}
, and hence to
\hat{F}
, as an
\mathcal{R}
-module; in particular
F
is free over
\mathcal{R}
.

We have obtained a short exact sequence of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
F\hookrightarrow M\twoheadrightarrow\bar{N}^{\vee}
, and we will prove it is split by checking that
M
retracts
\mathcal{A}
-linearly onto
F
. This will follow from proving that the
\mathcal{A}
-linear composition
\iota\colon F\hookrightarrow M\cong M^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow F^{\vee}
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces; by finite-dimensionality, it suffices to prove that
\iota
is a surjection. Furthermore, since
\iota
is
\mathcal{R}
-linear, it suffices to prove that
\iota
induces a surjection
F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F\twoheadrightarrow F^{\vee}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F^{\vee}
. Now notice that, since both
F
and
F^{\vee}
are free over
\mathcal{R}
, an element of either
F
or
F^{\vee}
is a multiple of
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
if and only if it is
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}
-torsion; thus it suffices to check that
\iota/\iota\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\right\}\mathclose{}
is surjective, but the latter is the composition

F/F\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\right\}\mathclose{}\to M/M\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\right\}\mathclose{}\to M^{\vee}/M^{\vee}\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\right\}\mathclose{}\to F^{\vee}/F^{\vee}\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\right\}\mathclose{},

where all maps are isomorphisms because
\bar{N}
and
\bar{N}^{\vee}
are
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}
-torsion. Thus we conclude that
\iota
is an
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism, proving that
F
is a split
\mathcal{A}
-submodule of
M
. Now let
N
be a direct complement of
F
, e.g. the image of a section of the
\mathcal{A}
-linear split surjection
M\twoheadrightarrow\bar{N}^{\vee}
.

It remains to check that
F
and
N
are tame over
\mathcal{A}
. We already have checked that
N\cong N^{\vee}
and
F\cong F^{\vee}
as
\mathcal{A}
-modules.
Let
k\geq 0
and let
\psi_{k}\colon\mathcal{F}_{k}\to M
be an
\mathcal{A}
-linear map as in Definition
8.5
, with
\mathcal{F}_{k}
free over
\mathcal{A}
.
By Remark
8.10
, we can assume that
\mathcal{F}_{k}
splits as a direct sum of free
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{F}
and
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{N}
, so that
\psi_{k}
restricts to maps
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{F}\to F
and
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{N}\to N
: here we use that the weighted
\mathbb{F}
-vector space
M/\mathcal{A}_{+}M
is the direct sum of the weighted
\mathcal{F}
-vector spaces
F/\mathcal{A}_{+}F
and
N/\mathcal{A}_{+}N
. If
\psi_{k}
is injective in weights at least
-k
, so must be its restrictions to
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{F}
and
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{N}
. This proves that
F
and
N
are tame over
\mathcal{A}
.
∎

8.4.
Propagation of tameness

Let
F
be an
\mathcal{A}
-module. Considering both
F
and
\mathbb{F}
as
\mathcal{R}
-modules, we compute

F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F\cong\mathrm{Tor}^{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(F,\mathbb{F})
.
Recall Notation
8.3
: we have
\mathcal{A}\cong\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, and in particular

F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F
inherits a
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
-module structure.

Proposition 8.19
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{Z}
and let
F
be a
u
-tame
\mathcal{A}
-module which is free over the ring
\mathcal{R}
. Then the module
F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F
is
(u-D_{1}+d_{0})
-tame over
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the case
u=0
, i.e.
F
is tame over
\mathcal{A}
, and prove that the shifted module
(F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F)[-D_{1}+d_{0}]
is tame over
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

Since
F
is free over
\mathcal{R}
, we have that the map
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\cdot-
induces a weight-preserving isomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F\cong F\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}\right\}\mathclose{}[2(D_{1}-d_{0})]
. On the other hand, it is easy to see that for
any

\mathcal{A}
-module
M
we have an isomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
M\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}\right\}\mathclose{}^{\vee}\cong M^{\vee}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}M^{\vee}
; using that
F
is tame over
\mathcal{A}
, we obtain a chain of
\mathcal{A}
-linear isomorphisms

\begin{split}(F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F[-D_{1}+d_{0}])^{\vee}&\cong(F\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}\right\}\mathclose{}[D_{1}-d_{0}])^{\vee}\cong F\mathopen{}\left\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}\right\}\mathclose{}^{\vee}[-D_{1}+d_{0}]\\
&\cong(F^{\vee}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F^{\vee})[-D_{1}+d_{0}]\cong(F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F)[-D_{1}+d_{0}].\end{split}

Next, let
k\geq D_{1}-d_{0}
, and let
\psi_{k}\colon\mathcal{F}_{k}\to F
be as in Definition
8.5
, with
\mathcal{F}_{k}
free over
\mathcal{A}
and generated in weights at least
k
. Then
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}:=\mathcal{F}_{k}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}\mathcal{F}_{k}
is free over
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
and is generated in weights at least
k
, and
\psi^{\prime}_{k}:=\psi_{k}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}\psi_{k}\colon\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}\to F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F
is a map of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
-modules and is surjective in weights at least
k
. We claim that
\psi^{\prime}_{k}
is injective in weights at least
-k+2(D_{1}-d_{0})
.

To prove this, let
\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{r}
be generators of
\mathcal{F}_{k}
over
\mathcal{A}
, and let
\bar{\nu}_{1},\dots,\bar{\nu}_{r}
be their projections on
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}
, which are generators of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}
over
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
. Let
m\geq-k+2(D_{1}-d_{0})
, and suppose that there are elements
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{r}\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, not all zero, such that the sum
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\lambda_{j}\psi^{\prime}_{k}(\bar{\nu}_{j})
is a homogeneous sum of weight
m
and vanishes in
F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F
. Then the element
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
is sent along
\psi_{k}
to an element divisible by
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
, hence this element must itself be divisible by
\mathfrak{y}_{0}
in
\mathcal{F}_{k}
. It follows that
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}
lies in the kernel of the map
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\cdot-\colon\mathcal{F}_{k}\to\mathcal{F}_{k}
, i.e.
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}
is a homogeneous sum of weight
m-2(D_{1}-d_{0})\geq-k
, and it vanishes: since at least one coefficient
\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{d_{1}-1}\lambda_{j}\in\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{R}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is non-zero, we get a contradiction.

The maps
\psi^{\prime}_{k}[-D_{1}+d_{0}]
witness that
F/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F[-D_{1}+d_{0}]
is tame over
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
∎

9.
Proof of Theorem
C
and its corollaries

In this section we prove Theorem
C
, together with its corollaries.
Throughout the section we let
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}(1,p,p,p,\dots)\cong\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)
as in Example
8.4
.

Definition 9.1

(Modules
N_{u,i}
)
.

Fix
u\geq 0
. We define recursively, for
i\geq 0
, an
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})
-module
N_{u,i}
.

Recall from Example
8.9
that the
\mathcal{A}
-module
B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
is
u
-tame. By Proposition
8.14
, we can decompose
B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
as a direct sum of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
F_{u,0}\oplus N_{u,0}
, with both modules being
u
-tame,
F_{u,0}
being free over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})
and
N_{u,0}
being narrow. We consider
N_{u,0}
as an
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})
-module, and recall that all variables
\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{2},\dots
act trivially on
N_{u,0}
. This is the base case.

Recursively, we assume to have defined, for some
i\geq 0
, a module
F_{u,i}
which is
(u-p^{i}+1)
-tame over the algebra

\mathcal{A}(p^{i},p,p,\dots)=\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots),

and such that
F_{u,i}
is free as a module over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})
; by Proposition
8.19
the module
F_{u,i}/\mathfrak{y}_{i}F_{u,i}
is
(u-p^{i+1}+1)
-tame over the algebra

\mathcal{A}(p^{i+1},p,p,\dots)=\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\mathfrak{y}_{i+2}\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\mathfrak{y}_{i+2}^{p},\dots),

and by Proposition
8.14
we have a decomposition of the module
F_{u,i}/\mathfrak{y}_{i}F_{u,i}
as a direct sum
F_{u,i+1}\oplus N_{u,i+1}
, with both modules being
(u-p^{i+1}+1)
-tame over
\mathcal{A}(p^{i+1},p,p,\dots)
,
F_{u,i+1}
being free over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p})
and
N_{u,i+1}
being
narrow
: in particular all variables
\mathfrak{y}_{i+2},\mathfrak{y}_{i+3},\dots
act trivially on
N_{u,i+1}
. We consider
N_{u,i+1}
as an
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}]
-module.

We remark that the isomorphism type of each
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})
-module
N_{u,i}
is unique, by Proposition
8.14
. Note also that the module
N_{u,i}
is concentrated in even weights contained in the interval
]-u-p^{i+1}+2p^{i}-1,-u+p^{i+1}-1[
.

Definition 9.2
.

For a finite-dimensional weighted
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector space
M
, we denote by
P_{M}(s)\in\mathbb{Z}[s^{\pm 1}]
the Poincaré polynomial of
M
, i.e. the Laurent polynomial

P_{M}(s)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}M(j))s^{j}

We notice that, for all
i\geq 0
, the Laurent polynomial
P_{N_{u,i}}(s)
has vanishing coefficients of all monomials
s^{j}
for
j\notin]-u-p^{i+1}+2p^{i}-1,-u+p^{i+1}-1[
. This, together with the following Lemma, characterises the polynomials
P_{N_{u,i}}(s)
.

Lemma 9.3
.

For
u\geq 0
, the Poincaré polynomials of the modules
N_{u,i}

satisfy the following equality:

(1+s^{-2})^{u}=\sum_{i\geq 0}P_{N_{u,i}}(s)\cdot\frac{s^{-2p^{i}}-1}{s^{-2}-1}=\sum_{i\geq 0}P_{N_{u,i}}(s)\cdot(1+s^{-2}+s^{-4}+\dots+s^{-2p^{i}+2}).

Proof.

We leave the proof to the reader.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

The sum in the right-hand side of the formula of Lemma
9.3
is a sum of Laurent polynomials with non-negative coefficients. Since the left hand side is a polynomial supported on monomials
s^{j}
for
j\in[-2u,0]
, which is an interval of width
2u
, there can be no summand in the right hand side contributing to the coefficients of two monomials
s^{j}
and
s^{j^{\prime}}
with
|j-j^{\prime}|>2u
. In particular, if
h
is such that
p^{h}-1\leq u\leq p^{h+1}-2
, then
N_{u,i}=0
for all
i>h
.

Proof of Theorem
C
.

We keep denoting
\mathcal{A}=\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)
.
The aim is to compute
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as a module over
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, and we do so by a recursion on increasingly deep submodules and the number of variables
\mathfrak{y}_{i}
involved.

We start by decomposing
B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
to
F_{u,0}\oplus N_{u,0}
as an
\mathcal{A}
-module. We have an isomorphism of

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-modules

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})\oplus\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(N_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p}),

so we focus on the computation of the two summands.

We compute
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(N_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})
using the Künneth isomorphism. The algebra
\mathcal{A}
is the tensor product of the algebras
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})
and
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)
. Correspondingly, the
\mathcal{A}
-module
N_{u,0}
(respectively,
\mathbb{F}_{p}
) is the tensor product of the
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})
-module
N_{u,0}
(respectively,
\mathbb{F}_{p}
) and the
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)
-module
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. We thus factor
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as the tensor product of the rings

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and, correspondingly,
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(N_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as the tensor product of the modules

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(N_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
. This accounts for the
0
th
direct summand in the statement of Theorem
C
.

Turning to the other summand
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, it follows from a reason similar to the previous paragraph that
\mathcal{A}
and the second module
\mathbb{F}_{p}
factor are tensor products of two rings and two modules, respectively. We may thus invoke the Künneth spectral sequence, which is supported on the quadrant
s,t\leq 0
and reads:

(9)

E^{2}_{s,t}=\mathrm{Ext}^{s}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathrm{Tor}_{-t}^{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p}),\mathbb{F}_{p})\Rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{s+t}_{\mathcal{A}}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p}).

Since
F_{u,0}
is a free
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})
-module, only the row
E^{2}_{s,0}
contains non-trivial entries, and in particular the spectral sequence collapses on the second page. The spectral sequence (
9
) is a module over the Künneth spectral sequence for
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, which also collapses on its second page.
By identifying
\mathrm{Tor}_{0}^{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})
with
F_{u,0}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F_{u,0}
as
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)
-modules, and using the ring decomposition

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p}),

we obtain an identification of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-modules

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{1}^{p},\dots)}(F_{u,0}/\mathfrak{y}_{0}F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p}).

In particular, all classes in
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
for
\star
strictly negative act trivially on
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(F_{u,0},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

We then continue recursively as follows. Suppose that, for some
i\geq 1
, we have reduced ourselves to computing
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(F_{u,i-1}/\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}F_{u,i-1},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as an
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module. We first decompose
F_{u,i-1}/\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}F_{u,i-1}
as a direct sum of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)
-modules
F_{u,i}\oplus N_{u,i}
. By Künneth, we obtain
an isomorphism of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-modules

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\\
&\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p}),\end{split}

which accounts for the
i
th
direct summand in the statement of Theorem
C
. The remaining summand is
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(F_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, to which we apply the Künneth spectral sequence; this is concentrated on the
0
th
row and thus collapses, so we identify

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(F_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots)}(F_{u,i}/\mathfrak{y}_{i}F_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p}).

The problem is reduced to computing
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots)}(F_{u,i}/\mathfrak{y}_{i}F_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as an
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module, which is left to the recursion.

The process eventually terminates by Remark
9.4
, completing the proof.
∎

In the rest of the section, we prove the Corollaries of Theorem
C
.

Proof of Corollary
C.1
.

The statement is a direct consequence of Theorems
B
, Proposition
6.11
, the isomorphism
2\!B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}\cong B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
and the fact that, for any finitely generated
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p}
-module
N
,
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p}}(N,\mathbb{F}_{p})
is finitely generated and free as an
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{i}]
-module.
∎

Proof of Corollary
C.2
.

By a standard analysis from homological algebra, for
i\geq 0
and for each finitely generated
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})
-module
M
, the
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})
is generated by the vector space

\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})=\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})\oplus\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p}).

We tensor both the ring
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and the module
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})
with the ring
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i+1},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i+1}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, and obtain that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})
generates the
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
We can even consider
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})
as an
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module by letting the ideal
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star<0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
act trivially.
This holds in particular for
M=N_{u,i}
:
therefore, for all
u\geq 0
and
i\geq 0
, the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
generates
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i},\dots]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p},\dots)}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
over the ring
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

It follows now from Theorem
C
that the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
generates
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
over
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
. For all
g\geq 0
we can now recall Definition
6.7
, and by taking a suitable direct sum we obtain that the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})
generates
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})
over
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
Basechanging to
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\epsilon]
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we obtain that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})
generates
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\epsilon]\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\epsilon]\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

Finally, tensoring both the modules and the generating vector space with the vector space
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]=\mathbb{F}_{p}[H_{1}(\Sigma_{g,1})]
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we obtain that

\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
generates
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\epsilon]\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
over the ring
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\epsilon]\otimes\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, which by Theorem
B
is identified with
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(D);\mathbb{F}_{p})
. We now observe that, according to the decomposition of Theorem
B
, the vector space

\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
is concentrated in bigradings
(\bullet,\star)
satisfying
-1\leq\star\leq 0
; it follows that

\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]\subseteq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}H_{n-1}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})\oplus\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}H_{n}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p}).

The statement of the corollary is now a direct consequence of Theorem
B
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
C.3
.

Theorem
B
ensures that
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})
is a basechange to
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\epsilon]\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
of the
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, so it suffices to study the latter. By Definition
6.7
,
\mathbb{M}_{g}
splits as a direct sum of shifted copies of
\mathcal{A}
-modules of the form
B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
, for values of
u
that are at most
g
.
We then have two cases.

•

If
g<p^{\lambda}
, then for all
u\leq g
we have that
N_{u,i}=0
for
i\geq\lambda+1
, whereas
N_{u,\lambda}
is a
\mathcal{A}
-module with trivial action of
\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda}
, or in other words,
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda}}(N_{u,\lambda})
consists solely of bars of size 1: this is because
N_{u,\lambda}
is supported in weights in the interval
[-2u,0]
, which is strictly narrower than
2p^{\lambda}
. It follows that
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda}^{p})}(N_{u,\lambda},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is a free
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
-module, and the claim now follows from Theorem
C
.

•

If
g<(p-1)p^{\lambda-1}
, then for all
u\leq g
we have that
N_{u,i}=0
for
i\geq\lambda
, and
N_{u,\lambda-1}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}^{p})
-module with no free summand, or in other words,
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}}(N_{u,\lambda-1})
consists solely of bars of size strictly less than
p
: this is because
N_{u,\lambda-1}
is supported in weights in the interval
[-2u,0]
, which is strictly narrower than
2(p-1)p^{\lambda-1}
. We now use the general fact that for a finitely generated
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}^{p})
-module
M
with no free summand, the module
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}^{p})}(M,\mathbb{F}_{p})
is free over the polynomial subalgebra
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{\lambda-1}]
of

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{\lambda-1}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
The claim follows again from Theorem
C
.

∎

Proof of Corollary
C.4
.

By Theorem
B
,
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1}))=H_{\bullet+\star}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1}))
is the tensor product of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
with a bigraded abelian group that is free abelian in each
bigrading, namely
\mathbb{Z}[\epsilon]\otimes\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
. Both bigraded abelian groups are concentrated in non-negative bar-degrees
\star\geq 0
. The part of

H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1}))
in bar-degree
\star=0
is therefore a direct sum of shifted copies of
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
. Similarly, the part of
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1}))
in bar-degree
\star=-1
is a direct sum of shifted copies of
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
. We will therefore prove that
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
is a free abelian group, and that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
only has 2-power torsion.

For the first statement, we note that
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
can be computed using the cobar complex

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes\star+1}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z}),

and in particular
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})
can be identified with the kernel of the differential

\delta^{0}\colon\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)\otimes\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathbb{M}_{g},\mathbb{Z}),

whose source is a free abelian group.

For the second statement, by Definition
6.7
it suffices to prove that the abelian group
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(2\!B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
has only
2
-power torsion for
u\geq 0
; after inverting 2, we can replace
2\!B^{\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}]}_{u}
by
B^{\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}]}_{u}
and aim to prove that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}]}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}]}_{u},\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}])
is a free
\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{2}]
-module. We will directly prove that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
is a free abelian group.

For this, we use again that
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
can be computed as the cohomology of the cobar complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes\star+1}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
. We want to argue that the differential

\delta^{0}\colon\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes 2}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})

has a split image, i.e.
\mathrm{Im}(\delta^{0})
is a direct summand of the target; once this is proved, we can find a direct complement
C\subset\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes 2}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
to
\mathrm{Im}(\delta^{0})
, and identify
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
with the kernel of the restriction to
C
of the differential

\delta^{-1}\colon\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes 2}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes 3}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z}),

thus proving that it is a free abelian group.

To prove that
\mathrm{Im}(\delta^{0})
is a summand, we can consider the
\mathbb{Z}
-dual chain complex, i.e. the bar complex
\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes\star+1}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u}
, and in particular the differential

d_{1}\colon\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes 2}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u}\to\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u};

it suffices to prove that
\mathrm{Im}(d_{1})
is a summand. Since the source and the target are free abelian groups, this is equivalent to proving that
\mathrm{Tor}_{0}^{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
is a free abelian group; this last statement follows from Proposition
7.11
, identifying the latter with the free abelian group spanned by all elements
z_{S}
for
S\subset\mathopen{}\left\{1,\dots,u\right\}\mathclose{}
sparse.
This completes the proof of the second statement.

The equality
\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}(H_{n}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{Q}))=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(H_{n}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p}))
is now a direct consequence of the universal coefficient theorem for homology.
∎

Proof of Corollary
C.5
.

For
n\geq 0
, the homology
H_{*}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{Z})
can be computed as the homology of a finitely generated chain complex of free abelian groups; such a chain complex can be decomposed as a direct sum of shifted copies of atomic chain complexes
0\to\mathbb{Z}\to 0

and
0\to\mathbb{Z}\overset{\cdot m}{\to}\mathbb{Z}\to 0
with
m\geq 1
.
A standard analysis of the behaviour of each type of atomic chain complex after tensoring with the short exact sequence
\mathbb{Z}\overset{\cdot p}{\to}\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{p}
leads to the equivalence of the following two statements:

(1)

for
n\geq 0
, all
p
-power torsion classes in
H_{*}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{Z})
are
p
-torsion;

(2)

for
n\geq 0
, all
p
-power torsion classes in
H_{*}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{Z})
are in the image of the Bockstein homomorphism
H_{*+1}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})\to H_{*}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{Z})
.

Thus, in order to prove Corollary
C.5
, it suffices to show (1). By Theorem
B
(1) is equivalent to the following statement (restricted to
0\leq u\leq g
):

(3)

for
u\geq 0
, all
p
-power torsion classes in
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
are
p
-torsion.

We next prove that (3) follows from the following statement:

(4)

for
u\geq 0
, all summands in
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}_{u},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2})
of the form
\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}
are in bar-degrees
-1\leq\star\leq 0
.

We use as in the proof of Corollary
C.4
that
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
is the cohomology of the cochain complex of free abelian groups
\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes\star+1}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
. Tensoring this cochain complex with
\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}
, we obtain the analogue cochain complex computing
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}_{u},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2})
. A standard analysis involving the universal coefficients theorem implies that for each of the following triples
(A,B,C)
, a summand of type
A
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{i}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
gives rise to a summand of type
B
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{i}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}_{u},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2})
and a summand of type
C
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{i+1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}_{u},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2})
:

(A,B,C)=\quad(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2},0),\quad(\mathbb{Z}/p,\mathbb{Z}/p,\mathbb{Z}/p),\quad(\mathbb{Z}/p^{l},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2})\mbox{ with }l\geq 2.

Combining with the observation that

\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
contains no
p
-power torsion, we deduce that (3) follows from (4).

We next take the Bockstein long exact sequence obtained by tensoring the cochain complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)^{\otimes\star+1}\otimes B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
with the short exact sequence
\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Let
\mathfrak{b}\colon\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{\star+1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
denote the Bockstein homomorphism; we regard
\mathfrak{b}
a differential on
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and consider the
\mathfrak{b}
-cohomology of the latter.
As before, we can decompose the cochain complex

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}(y)}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}(y)^{\otimes\star+1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}B^{\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}}_{u},\mathbb{Z}/p^{2})
as a direct sum of shifts of the atomic complexes
0\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\to 0
,
0\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\overset{\cdot p}{\to}\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\to 0
and
0\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\overset{\cdot 1}{\to}\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\to 0
. By a standard analysis, (4) is equivalent to the following statement:

(5)

for
u\geq 0
, the
\mathfrak{b}
-cohomology of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is concentrated in bar-degrees
-1\leq\star\leq 0
.

Denote by
-\cdot-
the Yoneda product: then for all
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ext}^{i}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and
\eta\in\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
we have the equality
\mathfrak{b}(\gamma\cdot\eta)=\mathfrak{b}(\gamma)\cdot\eta+(-1)^{i}\gamma\cdot\mathfrak{b}(\eta)
, which we call the “Leibniz rule” for the Bockstein homomorphism; a similar rule holds when
\eta\in\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
A standard computation gives
\mathfrak{b}(\alpha_{i+1})=\beta_{i}
and
\mathfrak{b}(\beta_{i})=0

for all
i\geq 0
; by the Leibniz rule, we get that the Bockstein homomorphism on
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is a map of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\dots]
-modules.

Let
S_{i}
be the
i
th
summand of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u};\mathbb{F}_{p})
as in Theorem
C
:

S_{i}=\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\bigotimes_{j=i+1}^{\infty}\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{j}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{j}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p}).

Then
\beta_{j}
annihilates
S_{i}
for all
0\leq j\leq i-1
, whereas
\beta_{j}
acts injectively on
S_{i}
for
i\leq j\leq\infty
. It follows that the Bockstein homomorphism
\mathfrak{b}
on
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
sends
S_{i}
to the direct sum
\bigoplus_{j=i}^{\infty}S_{j}
. Therefore the direct sums
\bigoplus_{j=i}^{\infty}S_{j}
, for varying
i\geq 0
, give a descending filtration of the Bockstein cochain complex
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
We denote by
\bar{S}_{i}=\mathopen{}\left(\bigoplus_{j=i}^{\infty}S_{j}\right)\mathclose{}/\mathopen{}\left(\bigoplus_{j=i+1}^{\infty}S_{j}\right)\mathclose{}
the
i
th
filtration quotient, and by
\bar{\mathfrak{b}}
its differential. Then (5) is implied by the following statement:

(6)

for
u\geq 0
and
i\geq 0
, the
\mathfrak{b}
-cohomology of
\bar{S}_{i}
is concentrated in bar-degrees
-1\leq\star\leq 0
.

We can regard
\bar{S}_{i}
as a differential bigraded module over the differential bigraded algebra
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\dots]
, which we endow with the differential
\bar{\mathfrak{b}}
defined by
\bar{\mathfrak{b}}(\alpha_{j+1})=\beta_{j}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{b}}(\beta_{j})=0
for
j\geq i
. Note that
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\dots]
is
\mathbb{F}_{p}
in bigrading (0,0), it is concentrated in bigradings
\bullet\geq 0
and
\star\leq 0
, and its module
\bar{S}_{i}
is freely generated by the bigraded vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

We define a monomial filtration on
\bar{S}_{i}
: for all
j\geq 0
, we put in filtration at least
j
all elements that can be written as the product of a class in
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and a monomial of at least
j
variables (counted with repetition) chosen among
\beta_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\dots
. For all
j\geq 0
, the part of
\bar{S}_{i}
of filtration at least
j
is a sub-cochain complex, and (6) is implied by the following statement

(7)

for
u\geq 0
,
i\geq 0
, the associated graded of
(\bar{S}_{i},\bar{\mathfrak{b}})
with respect to the monomial filtration has cohomology only in bar-degrees
-1\leq\star\leq 0
.

For the last statement, we notice that the associated graded of
\bar{S}_{i}
with respect to the monomial filtration is isomorphic, as a bigraded cochain complex, to the tensor product of the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, with trivial differential, and the algebra
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\dots]
, with differential
\bar{\mathfrak{b}}
. The
\bar{\mathfrak{b}}
-cohomology of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\dots]
is
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, sitting in bar-degree 0, and (7) follows.
∎

Proof of Corollary
C.6
.

By Theorem
B
it suffices to prove that for any
u\leq p-2
there
is an isomorphism of bigraded
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector spaces

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathopen{}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Q}}_{u},\mathbb{Q})\right)\mathclose{}^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{0},\alpha_{1},\beta_{1},\alpha_{2},\beta_{2}\dots];

applying part (2) of Corollary
C.3
with
\lambda=1
, it suffices to prove an isomorphism

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathopen{}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Q}}_{u},\mathbb{Q})\right)\mathclose{}^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{0}].

The barcode of
B^{\mathbb{Q}}_{u}
as a module over
\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(y)\cong\mathbb{Q}[y]
has the following description: for all
1\leq k\leq\lfloor u/2\rfloor
there are
\ell_{u,k}
bars of the form
(-2k,u+1-2k)
. The same description holds for the barcode of
B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})
: since
u\leq p-2
, the free-narrow decomposition of
B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u}
has only a narrow part.
It is then standard to prove, for
1\leq c\leq p-1
, that there is an isomorphism of bigraded vector spaces

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{0}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{0}^{c}),\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathopen{}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\mathbb{Q}[y]}(\mathbb{Q}[y]/(y^{c}),\mathbb{Q})\right)\mathclose{}^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{0}].

∎

Proof of Corollary
C.7
.

Using Theorem
B
and Proposition
6.11
, the claim is implied by the following statement:
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
has no
p
-torsion in homological degree
*=\bullet+\star<\max\mathopen{}\left\{2p-2,u+p\right\}\mathclose{}
.
As in the proof of Corollary
C.5
, the
p
-torsion in
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
coincides with the image of the Bockstein homomorphism
\mathfrak{b}^{\mathbb{Z}}\colon\mathrm{Ext}^{\star+1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
, and the natural map

\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is injective when restricted to the image of
\mathfrak{b}^{\mathbb{Z}}
. Denoting by

\mathfrak{b}\colon\mathrm{Ext}^{\star+1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})

the composite of
\mathfrak{b}^{\mathbb{Z}}
and the aforementioned natural map (i.e., the Bockstein homomorphism from the proof of Corollary
C.5
), we have to prove that the image of
\mathfrak{b}
is concentrated in homological degrees
*=\bullet+\star\geq\max\mathopen{}\left\{2p-2,u+p\right\}\mathclose{}
.

We observe that
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and so we replace the former by the latter.
By Theorem
C
, the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
can be identified with the direct sum
E\oplus E^{\prime}
, where

\begin{split}E=&\bigoplus_{i=0}^{h}\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p});\\
E^{\prime}=&\bigoplus_{i=0}^{h}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathopen{}\left(\bigoplus_{j=i}^{\infty}\mathbb{F}_{p}\cdot\alpha_{j+1}\right)\mathclose{}.\end{split}

According to Corollary
C.2
,
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is generated by the direct sum
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\oplus E\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as a module over
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

We proceed to analyse the behaviour of
\mathfrak{b}
on
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1\leq\star\leq 0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
. By Corollary
C.4
,
\mathfrak{b}
vanishes on
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, since the natural map
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
is surjective. Instead
\mathfrak{b}
is injective on
E^{\prime}
: since
\mathfrak{b}(\alpha_{j+1})=\beta_{j}
, we may identify the restriction of
\mathfrak{b}
on
E^{\prime}
with the injective composition of maps

\begin{split}E^{\prime}&=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathopen{}\left(\bigoplus_{j=i}^{\infty}\mathbb{F}_{p}\cdot\alpha_{j+1}\right)\mathclose{}\cong\\
&\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathopen{}\left(\bigoplus_{j=i}^{\infty}\mathbb{F}_{p}\cdot\beta_{j}\right)\mathclose{}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{-2}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p}).\end{split}

Finally, let
\mathfrak{b}_{-1}
denote the map
\mathfrak{b}\colon\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{-2}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
; then
\ker(\mathfrak{b}_{-1})
has the same total dimension as
E
. For this, first note that

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}E=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})=\binom{u}{\lfloor u/2\rfloor},

as we have ungraded isomorphisms

E=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{h}\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\bigoplus_{i=0}^{h}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p}).

Similarly, the total rank of
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Z}}_{u},\mathbb{Z})
is both equal to

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Q}}_{u},\mathbb{Q})=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{Q}}_{u},\mathbb{Q})=\binom{u}{\lfloor u/2\rfloor},

and to
\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\ker(\mathfrak{b}_{-1})
, by Corollary
C.4
).

Combining the previous remarks, we conclude that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
also decomposes as the direct sum
E^{\prime}\oplus\ker(\mathfrak{b}_{-1})
. In particular, every element in
E
can be written as a sum of an element in
E^{\prime}
and an element in
\ker(\mathfrak{b})
.

We will now estimate the homological degrees in which a non-zero element in the image of
\mathfrak{b}\colon\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{\star-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
may occur. We will consider two types of additive generators of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
: the first type comes from
\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, and the second type from
E
.

Fix
0\leq i\leq h
, let
x\in\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and let
s
be a bihomogeneous element of bidegree
(\bullet_{s},\star_{s})
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\beta_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\dots]\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})
; then
s\cdot x
is a generator of first type of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
, and

\mathfrak{b}(s\cdot x)=\mathfrak{b}(s)\cdot x
. Assuming that
\mathfrak{b}(s)
is non-zero, we have that
s
belongs to the ideal generated by the variables
\alpha_{j}
for
j\geq i+1
; in particular
\star_{s}\leq-1
and
\bullet_{s}\geq p^{i+1}(-\star_{s}+1)
. Using that
N_{u,i}
is supported in weights less than
-u+p^{i+1}-1
, we have that
x
has weight
\bullet_{x}>u-p^{i+1}+1
. Thus
\mathfrak{b}(s)\cdot x
has bar-degree
\star_{s}-1
and weight
\bullet_{x}+\bullet_{s}>u-p^{i+1}+1+p^{i+1}(-\star_{s}+1)
. It follows that the homological degree of
\mathfrak{b}(s)\cdot x
is strictly larger than

u+p-1
. Replacing the estimate
\bullet_{x}>u-p^{i+1}+1
with the estimate
\bullet_{x}>-1
, we obtain that the homological degree of
\mathfrak{b}(s)\cdot x
is also strictly larger than
2p-3
.

Fix now
0\leq i\leq h
, let
x\in\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathfrak{y}_{i}]/(\mathfrak{y}_{i}^{p})}(N_{u,i},\mathbb{F}_{p})
and let
s
be as above; then
s\cdot x
is a generator of second type of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
. We can decompose
x
inside
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
as a sum
x^{\prime}+x^{\prime\prime}
, with
x^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime\prime}
having the same weight as
x
, with
x^{\prime}\in\ker(\mathfrak{b})
, and with
x^{\prime\prime}\in E^{\prime}
, in particular
x^{\prime\prime}
lies in the image of the multiplication map

\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(\mathbb{F}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p}).

We get a splitting
\mathfrak{b}(s\cdot x)=\mathfrak{b}(s)\cdot x^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}(s\cdot x^{\prime\prime})
. The second term vanishes unless it lives in homological degree at least
\max\mathopen{}\left\{u+p,2p-2\right\}\mathclose{}
, by the previous argument. The first term can also be treated as in the previous argument: we now have
\star_{x}=-1
instead of 0, but we also have the better estimate
\bullet_{x}>\max\mathopen{}\left\{u-p^{i+1}+1,-1\right\}\mathclose{}+2p^{i}
.
∎

Remark 9.5
.

The fact that
\mathrm{Ext}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
can be decomposed as the direct sum of
E^{\prime}
and
\ker(\mathfrak{b})
gives a slight improvement of the statement of Corollary
C.2
:
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})
is generated over
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(D);\mathbb{F}_{p})
by the direct sum of

\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}H_{n}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})\ \mbox{ and }\ \ker(\mathfrak{b})\subset\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}H_{n-1}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p}).

Proof of Corollary
C.8
.

We fix
g\geq 1
and
i\geq 0
throughout the proof. A direct consequence of Theorems
B
and
C
is that
H_{n-i}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})\neq 0
as soon as
n\geq i
; hence it suffices to find constants
0<\mathbf{c}_{g,i}<\mathbf{C}_{g,i}
that work for
n
large enough.

We start by proving the existence of
\mathbf{c}_{g,i}
. Let
n=2m+\delta
with
\delta\in\mathopen{}\left\{0,1\right\}\mathclose{}
, and let
\xi\colon\mathopen{}\left\{1,\dots,g\right\}\mathclose{}\to\mathbf{K}
(recall Notation
6.10
) be the map defined by

\xi(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathbf{e}&\mbox{ if }j\leq g-1\mbox{ or }\delta=0;\\
\mathbf{u}&\mbox{ else}.\end{array}\right.

By Theorem
B
and Definition
6.7
, together with the isomorphism
B^{p}_{|\xi|}\cong 2\!B^{p}_{|\xi|}
, we have that

H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})
contains a copy of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{p}_{g-\delta},\mathbb{F}_{p})[\delta]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
; we will estimate the dimension of the latter in bigrading
(\bullet,\star)=(n,-i)
; setting
u=g-\delta
, and using
\delta=n-2m
, we get that
\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}H_{n-i}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})
is at least the dimension in bigrading
(2m,-i)
of
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
.

Let now
l:=\lfloor\log_{p}(m/(i+1))\rfloor\asymp\log_{p}(n)
,
and let
h:=\lfloor\log_{p}(u)\rfloor
.
Fix a non-trivial class
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
. For every subset
S\subset\mathopen{}\left\{h+1,\dots,l\right\}\mathclose{}
of cardinality
i
we can construct a non-trivial class
(\prod_{s\in S}\alpha_{s})\cdot\gamma\in\mathrm{Ext}^{-i}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})
of weight
w(S):=\sum_{s\in S}2p^{s}\leq 2ip^{l}\leq 2m/(i+1)
. We can “complete” this class by a monomial in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
of weight
2m-w(S)\geq m/(i+1)
, to get a class in
\mathrm{Ext}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
of bigrading
(2m,-i)
: such monomial can be chosen in
\binom{m-w(S)/2+2g-1}{2g-1}\asymp n^{2g-1}
ways.
Since there are
\binom{l-h}{i}\asymp\log_{p}(n)^{i}
ways to choose the subset
S
, we have produced
\asymp n^{2g-1}\log_{p}(n)^{i}
linearly independent classes in
H_{n-i}(C_{n}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})
. This proves the existence of
\mathbf{c}_{g,i}
.

We next prove the existence of
\mathbf{C}_{g,i}
. By Theorem
B
, the weighted vector space
H_{\bullet-i}(C_{\bullet}(\Sigma_{g,1});\mathbb{F}_{p})
can be expressed as a finite direct sum of shifted copies of
\mathrm{Ext}^{-j}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
for values of
j
and
u
satisfying
0\leq j\leq i
and
u\leq g
: multiplication by
\epsilon^{i-j}
puts such a copy in bar-degree
-i
. The number of these summands is bounded above by
2^{g}\cdot i
, so we can focus on the growth of the weighted dimension of
\mathrm{Ext}^{-j}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
, for fixed
0\leq j\leq i
and
u\geq 0
.

By Theorem
C
,
\mathrm{Ext}^{-j}_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(y)}(B^{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{u},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
further decomposes as a finite direct sum of suitable bigraded shifts of quotients of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\alpha_{1},\beta_{1},\dots]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
, so we can fix
j\leq i
and study the growth of the weighted dimension of the part of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
of bar-degree
-j
. Since
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
is concentrated in even weights, and we are looking for an upper bound on the dimension of the part of bigrading
(n,-j)
, for fixed
j
and varying
n
, of the form
\mathbf{C}_{g,i}\log_{p}(n)^{i}n^{2g-1}
, we can focus on the even case
n=2m
.

In order to form a monomial of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
of bigrading
(2m,-j)
, we need to
choose a monomial in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots]
of some weight
2w\leq 2m
and of bar-degree
-j
, and complete it by a suitable monomial in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
of weight
2m-2w
and bar-degree
0
. Letting
l=\lfloor\log_{p}(m)\rfloor
, only the variables
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots,\alpha_{l}
can be used for the first choice; an upper bound to the number of monomials is thus the number of
ordered
sequences of
j
letters in the alphabet
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots,\alpha_{l}
, which is
(2l+1)^{j}\asymp\log_{p}(n)^{j}=O(\log_{p}(n)^{i})
. For each choice of a monomial of weight
2w\leq 2m
, we can then choose a monomial of weight
2m-2w\leq 2m
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
in at most
\binom{m+2g-1}{2g-1}\asymp n^{2g-1}
ways. Thus the weighted dimension of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\dots]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}[\mathcal{H}_{g}]
in bar-degree
-j
is
O(n^{2g-1}\log_{p}(n)^{i})
.
This proves the existence of
\mathbf{C}_{g,i}
.
∎
